# The division of 1 Corinthians into reading units: a record of what was disputed

This record is generated mechanically from the reports of the readers who examined a proposed division of 1 Corinthians into 70 reading units. It is written for a reader of the book, not for a reader of the project that produced it: passages are named by chapter and verse.

Every quotation set off with a rule at the left is a reader's own sentence, altered only by the mechanical substitutions listed in full near the end. Every other sentence is the generator's own frame, and every such sentence is printed in the last section so that the frame can be inspected apart from the evidence.

This record makes no judgement of its own. It changes no unit, weighs no boundary and settles no dispute; it reports what the readers wrote. Its limits are stated in section 2 and are not small.

## 1. What made this book difficult

1 Corinthians runs to 437 verses in 16 chapters, divided here into 70 reading units. The verse numbering of the witnesses is stated by the section-mark layer in these words:

> WEB = RP = SBLGNT in every verse (437), counted in a working file. Every ref is WEB.

The reading plan chose a shape for the book before any unit was written. It set out that shape and the shapes it rejected in these words:

> **Adopted shape: an argument-structure tiling, with the topic headings as evidence grade-1 onsets and the epistolary frame as bracketing parents.** Thirteen parents, six drafter parts. The parents follow the letter's argumentative units:

> - an opening frame;

> - the divisions section;

> - the reported-conduct units;

> - the two halves of the marriage answers;

> - the idol-food argument;

> - the two worship-assembly units;

> - the spiritual-gifts argument;

> - the resurrection argument;

> - the business of the last chapter;

> - a closing frame.

> Evidence for, in evidence grade order:

> - Topic headings. The formula peri de opens a topic at six verses in both witnesses: 7:1, 7:25, 8:1, 12:1, 16:1 and 16:12 (read from the source, device inventory the formula “περὶ δέ at a sentence start (verse-initial after a stop, or after . ; · within the verse): the topic-marker reading” rp 6 / sbl 6). Five of them sit on parent seams adopted here. The sixth, 16:12, sits inside the travel-and-commendation business and is a entry onset only.

> - Frame formulae. They bracket the body in both witnesses: prescript 1:1, address 1:2, greeting 1:3, thanksgiving 1:4; then third-person greetings 16:19 and 16:20, the autograph 16:21, and the grace benediction 16:23 (read from the source, device inventory frame family; FRAME edges opening before 1:10, closing from 16:19).

> - Closing devices. Units whose front seam carries no heading are still closed on the back side by a text device:

> - the calling refrain at 7:24, "μενέτω παρὰ θεῷ.";

> - the imitation command at 11:1, which echoes 10:33 ("καθὼς κἀγὼ" ... "καθὼς κἀγὼ χριστοῦ."; counted, a working file: the phrase occurs at 10:33 and 11:1 only);

> - the order maxim at 14:40;

> - the hoste-plus-vocative exhortation at 15:58 ("Ὥστε, ἀδελφοί μου ἀγαπητοί,"; counted, a working file: a verse-initial hoste with the vocative occurs at 11:33, 14:39 and 15:58 only).

> - Units whose front carries no heading open with other formulae:

> - 1:10, the appeal formula "Παρακαλῶ δὲ ὑμᾶς, ἀδελφοί,";

> - 5:1, the report formula "Ὅλως ἀκούεται", asyndetic;

> - 6:1, an asyndetic question;

> - 11:2, the praise formula "Ἐπαινῶ δὲ ὑμᾶς, ἀδελφοί,";

> - 11:17, the negated praise "Τοῦτο δὲ παραγγέλλων οὐκ ἐπαινῶ,";

> - 15:1, the disclosure formula "Γνωρίζω δὲ ὑμῖν, ἀδελφοί,".

> Rivals tested:

> 1. **Rival A, topical two-block** (reported matters, then the matters of the Corinthians' letter from 7:1).

> - For: 7:1 names the letter, "Περὶ δὲ ὧν ἐγράψατέ μοι,". The headings are the strongest formula family in the book.

> - Against: the headings cannot tile the book. 11:2-11:34 and 15:1-15:58 carry no peri de: they open with the praise and disclosure formulae. 7:25 and 16:12 fall inside topics. 8:4 resumes with a bare peri, "Περὶ τῆς βρώσεως οὖν", inside the 8:1 topic (read from the source, device inventory the formula “bare περί (not περὶ δέ) at a sentence start” 8:4).

> - Retained as evidence grade-1 onsets; rejected as the tiling.

> 2. **Rival C, frame-only** (prescript, thanksgiving, one body, closing).

> - For: the frame formulae are measured in both witnesses.

> - Against: a single body parent of more than four hundred verses gives review no handle and hides every seam.

> - Retained as the two frame parents.

> 3. **Rival D, chapter-level parents** (separate parents at 2:1, 3:1, 9:1, 10:1, 13:1 and 14:1).

> - For: several of these verses carry a vocative or a disclosure formula; 10:1 has "Οὐ θέλω δὲ ὑμᾶς ἀγνοεῖν, ἀδελφοί,".

> - Against, for 8:1-11:1: the text ties chapters 8, 9 and 10 together. The idol-food word eidolothyt- (transliterated gloss) stands at 8:1, 8:4, 8:7 and 8:10 and returns at 10:19 and 10:28; syneidesis (conscience) stands at 8:7, 8:10 and 8:12 and returns at 10:25, 10:27, 10:28 and 10:29; exousia (right) at 8:9 is carried into chapter 9 at 9:4, 9:5, 9:6, 9:12 and 9:18; eleuther- (free) stands at 9:1, 9:19 and 10:29 (counted, an earlier a working file: accent-stripped stems on RP, counted within 8:1-11:1; blind check a numbered rule's a working file gives the same verses).

> - Against, for separate parents at 2:1 and 3:1: the naming of Apollos at 1:12 resumes at 3:4, 3:5, 3:6, 3:22 and 4:6, and of Kephas at 1:12 resumes at 3:22 (counted, an earlier a working file, counted within 1:10-4:21).

> - Corroboration only: the scorer's boundary-risk record says 1:10-4:21, 8:1-11:1 and 12:1-14:40 are each one argument and that a parent seam at 9:1 or 10:1 severs the idol-food argument (reported, hardness batch 03). It is not the ground of the adoption.

> - Against, for 12-14: the text gives a measured bracket: the imperative zeloute stands at 12:31 and 14:1 and again at 14:39 (counted, a working file), framing chapter 13 as an inset.

> - Against, for 8:1-11:1: the 11:1 echo of 10:33 closes 10:31-11:1, so 8:1-11:1 ends as one unit.

> - Rejected; these verses become entry-onset candidates (a section of the reading plan, a section of the reading plan).

> 4. **Rival E, one parent for 5:1-6:20.**

> - For: the topic porneia returns at 6:12-6:20 (interpretive from the WEB sense, not measured).

> - Against: 6:1 opens asyndetically on a new topic, lawsuits. The question ouk oidate (transliterated gloss; WEB "Don’t you know") binds 6:1-6:20: six of its ten hits fall in chapter 6, at 6:2, 6:3, 6:9, 6:15, 6:16 and 6:19 (read from the source, device inventory the formula “οὐκ οἴδατε ὅτι”). That gives chapter 6 its own cohesion.

> - Rejected. The 5:13/6:1 seam is graded B in a section of the reading plan

> 5. **Rival F, one parent for 11:2-11:34.**

> - For: both units concern the assembly; the verb synerchesthai appears at 11:17, 11:18, 11:20, 11:33 and 11:34 (counted, a working file; also at 7:5, 14:23 and 14:26; SBL lacks 7:5).

> - Against: the praise / negated-praise pair, ἐπαινῶ at 11:2 and οὐκ ἐπαινῶ at 11:17 (read from the source, device inventory epaino 11:2, 11:17, 11:22), gives a evidence grade-1 onset at 11:17. The appeal to the churches' custom at 11:16 closes the head-covering unit.

> - Rejected. Two parents.

The plan named in advance the regions it expected to be hardest, and graded what it expected of each:

> Grades: **A**, a evidence grade-1 text signal on both faces; **B**, a evidence grade-1 signal on one face with the other face inferred from sense or a weaker device; **C**, contested, so the entry discloses the rival reading. The grades are expectations for the review readings (interpretive).

> **A. 8:1-11:1 idol-food argument with the ch 9 digression** (scrutiny target 1).

> - One parent, parent section 07 8:1-11:1 (grade A).

> - Entries may open at 9:1, "Οὐκ εἰμὶ ἀπόστολος; Οὐκ εἰμὶ ἐλεύθερος;" (asyndetic questions, SBL order inverted), and at 10:1, "Οὐ θέλω δὲ ὑμᾶς ἀγνοεῖν, ἀδελφοί,". Each such entry states that it lies inside the idol-food argument (grades B).

> - 10:14 combines Dioper with the vocative "my beloved"; RP carries a pilcrow there (B).

> - 10:23 repeats the slogan (a numbered rule) (B).

> - 10:31-11:1 closes the argument, and 11:1 belongs with it by the echo of 10:33. The chapter division at 11:1 and the WEB paragraph start at 11:1 never drive (11:1/11:2, A).

> - Divided readings: none are recorded by the scorer for this region. Entries report the ch 9 function neutrally (a section of the reading plan).

> **B. 12:1-14:40 with ch 13 inset and 14:33b-36 text-critical block** (scrutiny target 2).

> - One parent, parent section 10 12:1-14:40 (A).

> - 12:31 holds two clauses: "Ζηλοῦτε δὲ" ..., then "Καὶ ἔτι καθ’ ὑπερβολὴν ὁδὸν ὑμῖν δείκνυμι.". The first clause matches 14:1 "Διώκετε τὴν ἀγάπην· ζηλοῦτε δὲ"; the second points forward. Entries are verse-granular, so 12:31 goes with chapter 12, and the entry discloses that 12:31b introduces chapter 13 (C). This is a working answer, listed for the final reviewer's final review.

> - Chapter 13 is an inset entry or entries (13:1, 13:4, 13:8; 13:13 closes) (B).

> - 14:33/14:34: the asyndetic new subject at 14:34 is a text signal; the RP pilcrow informs (C). Both entries meeting at the 14:33/14:34 seam (the entry ending at 14:33, and the entry opening at 14:34, which runs to 14:36 or, if a drafter finds no signal at 14:36/14:37, through 14:40) carry three disclosures:

> - the 14:33b attachment;

> - the SBL variant sites;

> - the transposition (a section of the reading plan).

> The rhetorical question at 14:36, "Ἢ ἀφ’ ὑμῶν ὁ", attaches backward (interpretive; a working answer, listed for the final reviewer's final review).

> - 14:36/14:37: 14:37 opens a conditional, "If any man thinks"; RP pilcrow (B).

> - Divided readings (reported, hardness batch 03): cessationist and continuationist readings of 12-14; readings of to teleion (transliterated gloss; WEB "that which is complete") at 13:8-13:12; 14:34-14:35 in debates over ordination. Entries name each reading and its holders without preferring one.

> **C. 11:2-16 headship unit and 11:17-34 Supper tradition** (scrutiny target 3).

> - Two parents, parent section 08 11:2-11:16 and parent section 09 11:17-11:34. The seam 11:16/11:17 is graded A: the churches' custom closes, and the negated praise opens.

> - 11:22/11:23: the tradition opens "Ἐγὼ γὰρ παρέλαβον ἀπὸ τοῦ κυρίου", but its gar ties it to 11:22 (B). A single entry 11:17-11:34 is acceptable.

> - 11:26/11:27 is never a entry seam (a numbered rule).

> - The 11:24 witness difference is disclosed (a section of the reading plan).

> - Divided readings (reported): kephale and head coverings, read differently across traditions; the Eucharist at 11:23-11:34, read in Catholic, Orthodox, Lutheran, Reformed and memorialist ways. Each reading is named and attributed, none is preferred, and none moves a seam.

> **D. 15:1-58 creed 15:3-7, sequence 15:20-28, mystery 15:50-58** (scrutiny target 4).

> - One parent, parent section 11 15:1-15:58 (A).

> - The creed "Παρέδωκα γὰρ ὑμῖν ἐν πρώτοις," and the apostle's appended witness 15:8-15:11 stay in one entry 15:1-15:11 (a numbered rule) (A).

> - 15:12 is a conditional; RP pilcrow (B).

> - 15:20 opens "But now", nyni de; RP pilcrow (B). The sequence 15:20-15:28 holds; the 15:23 "each in his own order" is interior.

> - 15:29 opens Epei; RP pilcrow (B).

> - 15:35, "Ἀλλ’ ἐρεῖ τις,", is an objection opener; RP pilcrow (B).

> - 15:50 against 15:51: "Τοῦτο δέ φημι, ἀδελφοί," against "Ἰδού, μυστήριον ὑμῖν λέγω·". 15:50 is pilcrow-only, and touto de phemi opens at 7:29 as well. The working answer opens a entry at 15:50, and the entry discloses the backward reading (C).

> - 15:57 "thanks be to God" and 15:58 close (A).

> - Divided reading (reported): baptism for the dead at 15:29, read differently in Latter-day Saint practice and in Catholic, Orthodox and Protestant readings. Named, attributed, not adjudicated.

> **E. slogan quotations 6:12-20, 7:1, 8:1-8, 10:23** (scrutiny target 5).

> - The extent convention is a numbered rule (a section of the reading plan).

> - counted (a working file quotes): 35 WEB verses carry double quotes. The WEB places slogan quotes at 6:12, 6:13, 6:18 and 10:23, and none at 7:1, 8:1, 8:4 or 8:8. This WEB punctuation is excluded from every extent decision.

> - Text signals for extent:

> - the repeated wording: "Πάντα μοι ἔξεστιν," at 6:12 and 10:23 in RP;

> - the adversative that begins the response, "ἀλλ’ οὐ πάντα συμφέρει·";

> - at 6:13, "Τὰ βρώματα τῇ κοιλίᾳ,", the extent of the second slogan is itself contested: it may end after the first pair (after tois bromasin, transliterated gloss) or run through katargesei. The text signals are the de of ho de theos, which may open the response, and the parallel future at 6:14 (ho de theos ... exegerei), which answers katargesei.

> - At 6:13 (the second slogan), at 7:1 ("it is good for a man not to touch a woman"), 8:1 ("οἴδαμεν ὅτι"), 8:4 and 8:8, whether a slogan is quoted, and how far, is contested.

> - Entries keep the candidate slogan and its response together and disclose "slogan extent contested" with the candidate extents.

> - Expected grade per slogan entry: B, with disclosure mandatory.

> - Divided readings (reported): ethics debates over 6:9-6:11. Named and attributed only.

> **F. ch 7 topical sub-seams 7:1, 7:8, 7:10, 7:12, 7:25, 7:36** (scrutiny target 6).

> - Two parents, parent section 05 7:1-7:24 and parent section 06 7:25-7:40.

> - 7:1 is the heading (A).

> - 7:8, "Λέγω δὲ τοῖς ἀγάμοις", is pilcrow-only and opens the addressee series (B).

> - 7:10 "to the married" and 7:12 "to the rest" continue that series (B each). The 7:10-7:11 command cites the Lord, and 7:12 marks "I—not the Lord—". The two entries stay distinct.

> - 7:17-7:24 is closed by the calling refrain (7:20, 7:24). 7:24/7:25 is graded A.

> - 7:36 opens a conditional case (B).

> - Divided reading (reported): divorce and the Pauline privilege at 7:10-7:16. Named and attributed only.

> **G. 16:1-24 collection, travel, greetings, maranatha** (scrutiny target 7).

> - Two parents, parent section 12 16:1-16:18 and parent section 13 16:19-16:24.

> - 15:58/16:1 is graded A.

> - 16:12, "Περὶ δὲ Ἀπολλὼ τοῦ ἀδελφοῦ,", is a entry onset, not a parent seam (B).

> - The closing edge is contested: 16:19 is adopted on the greeting formula. 16:13 begins imperatives, and 16:15 is an appeal followed by a commendation. 16:18/16:19 is graded B and goes to the final reviewer.

> - 16:21, the autograph "Ὁ ἀσπασμὸς τῇ ἐμῇ χειρὶ Παύλου.", is a entry onset inside parent section 13.

> - 16:22, curse and maranatha, is disclosed per a section of the reading plan

> - 16:23 is the benediction. 16:24 is the love closing; RP adds the amen.

> **H. Boundary risks in the hardness entry, each disposed:**

> - 1:10-4:21 as one section: one parent, parent section 02.

> - 8:1-11:1 with ch 9 embedded: one parent, parent section 07; no parent seam at 9:1 or 10:1.

> - 12-14 with 13 inset: one parent, parent section 10.

> - 15 as one argument: one parent, parent section 11.

> - Slogan-refutation pairs: a numbered rule and a numbered rule.

> - Tradition-plus-application blocks: a numbered rule.

> **I. My own finds:**

> - a numbered rule. 1:17/1:18. 1:17, "For Christ sent me not to baptize", hinges the divisions report to the word of the cross. 1:18 is pilcrow-only and no WEB start. Choose one entry seam, at 1:17 or 1:18, and disclose the hinge (C).

> - a numbered rule. 4:14-4:21. A paternal admonition with Timothy's sending (4:17) and the threatened visit (4:18-4:21). It closes parent section 02; a drafter may make it one entry. It is not a parent of its own, because nothing opens at 4:14 except the RP pilcrow and a self-referential statement about the writing, "I don’t write these things to shame you" (B).

> - a numbered rule. 5:13/6:1. The citation-imperative close and the asyndetic new topic (B). The vice lists of 5:10-5:11 and 6:9-6:10 tie the parents in sense (interpretive). The entry at 6:9-6:11 discloses the link.

> - a numbered rule. 9:1 wording. The question order is inverted between the witnesses (a section of the reading plan). Any WEB quotation discloses it.

> - a numbered rule. 14:39-14:40. 14:39 repeats zeloute and the hoste-vocative closer. 14:40 closes the parent with the order maxim, and SBL adds de at 14:40, a variant site.

> - a numbered rule. Divided readings outside the targets (reported): purgatory and 3:10-3:15; discipline at 5:1-5:13. Named and attributed only.

The two Greek witnesses are not one text. They differ in 218 of the 437 verses, and the secondary witness carries 419 critical signs in 220 verses. The section-mark layer describes these differences in these words:

> verses whose RP and SBLGNT wording differ after greek lib.skeleton (accents, case, punctuation and sigla removed). counted; quote the RP primary and disclose the SBL reading where a entry's argument rests on the wording.

> SBLGNT inline critical signs per verse. Each marks a textual-variant site in SBLGNT's own apparatus (the apparatus itself, set audit condition 2, stays local and is not read here). A entry spanning one discloses it; a boundary is never argued from a variant alone.

The witnesses mark paragraphs differently: the section-mark layer counts 35 paragraph starts in the primary Greek witness and 87 in the English, agreeing on 31. Its notes:

> Byzantine RP pilcrows, each at the start of the verse that begins a paragraph in that edition. Edition paragraphing is evidence: it informs a boundary and never decides one alone (a logged error).

> WEB \p/\m/\nb/\pi markers: evidence grade-4 translation formatting, VISIBLE so it can be excluded knowingly. It never drives a boundary (a logged error).

The formula inventory states these rules for using its counts:

> RULE: these are EVIDENCE, not a verdict. No boundary is set by a count alone; edition punctuation, edition paragraphing and translation formatting never drive one (a logged error).

> RULE: a refrain CLOSES a unit, so a closing formula (grace benediction, ἀμήν, a doxology) belongs to the unit BEFORE it.

> RULE: a seam is assessed from BOTH sides; an onset-only diagnosis is incomplete until the adjacent unit's close is weighed from bytes.

> RULE: refs are WEB = Greek (identity); assert the face anyway before comparing.

> RULE: quote RP (primary); where RP and SBLGNT disagree on a hit, disclose both and argue from neither alone. A disagreement whose cause is 'position class only' is an edition-punctuation fact, not a textual variant.

> RULE: a sentence start position rests on the edition's punctuation; it is evidence about the edition, not about the author.

> HYPOTHESIS to test: a sentence-initial περὶ δέ opens a topic section; weigh each against the close of the section before it, and weigh a mid-clause περὶ δέ separately.

> HYPOTHESIS to test: disclosure (οὐ θέλω ὑμᾶς ἀγνοεῖν, γνωρίζω ὑμῖν) and appeal (παρακαλῶ ὑμᾶς) formulae open units; test each from both sides.

> HYPOTHESIS to test: the vocative ἀδελφοί marks a turn or an appeal, but it is too frequent to set a boundary alone; the skeleton cannot separate vocative from nominative.

> HYPOTHESIS to test: citation formulae (γέγραπται, λέγει) and rhetorical οὐκ οἴδατε introduce support inside a unit and are weak boundary signals.

> HYPOTHESIS to test: the epistolary frame (prescript, salutation, thanksgiving, greetings, benediction) brackets the body; the thanksgiving verb also stands outside the frame, so the verb alone does not mark it.

> HYPOTHESIS to test: question openers (τί οὖν, τί γάρ, μὴ γένοιτο) step an argument within a unit.

The inventory records the following patterns. Each line gives its description, its family and its count in the primary and in the secondary Greek witness.

- “ἀδελφοί, any case reading (nominative and vocative share the form)” (vocative): 22 in the primary witness, 21 in the secondary
- “ἀδελφοί μου” (vocative): 3 in the primary witness, 4 in the secondary
- “ἀδελφοί μου ἀγαπητοί” (vocative): 1 in the primary witness, 1 in the secondary
- “ἀδελφοί not preceded by the article οἱ in the verse (the vocative reading; a heuristic)” (vocative): 20 in the primary witness, 19 in the secondary
- “adscript τῇ ἐκκλησίᾳ τοῦ θεοῦ” (frame): 2 in the primary witness, 2 in the secondary
- “ἀγαπητοί, any position” (vocative): 2 in the primary witness, 2 in the secondary
- “ἀγαπητοί μου” (vocative): 1 in the primary witness, 1 in the secondary
- “ἀμήν” (frame): 2 in the primary witness, 1 in the secondary

- “day noun ἡμέρα, any form” (date family control): 7 in the primary witness, 7 in the secondary
- “ἢ οὐκ οἴδατε 'or do you not know'” (rhetorical): 2 in the primary witness, 4 in the secondary

- “ἐπαινῶ δὲ ὑμᾶς” (praise): 1 in the primary witness, 1 in the secondary
- “εὐχαριστῶ 'I give thanks', any position” (frame): 4 in the primary witness, 4 in the secondary

The division as it stands records its own grades of confidence: high, 3; low, 1; medium, 47; medium-low, 19. Its unit types are: appeal, 3; argument step, 31; example, 5; exhortation, 9; frame closing, 3; frame prescript, 1; frame thanksgiving, 1; inset, 3; objection reply, 1; report response, 2; slogan with response, 2; topic response, 6; tradition with application, 2; travel and commendation, 1.

## 2. How the division was examined, and what that does and does not establish

The examination left these reports: 23 first-reading reports with 158 findings; 5 reviews between revision rounds with 52 new findings; 16 revision logs with 385 recorded changes; 2 audits; and 7 adjudication rounds with 131 rulings (adopt, 67; amend, 2; close, 10; defer, 2; ratify, 7; ratify with changes, 42; reverse, 1).

Each first reading stated its own role. The distinct statements are:

> primary reading A

> primary reading B

> primary reading C

What this does not establish. Every reading at every stage was produced by one family of language model. Where two readings agree, that is one voice heard twice, not two independent witnesses, and agreement in this record must never be taken as confirmation. No report by a human scholar is among the listed files. Whether any reader consulted anything beyond the witnesses named in section 8 is not tested by this record.

The readings were not all of one kind. Reading A examined 10 of the 10 groups of units, Reading B 10 and Reading C 3.

No reconciling reading exists for this book: where first readings differ, this record shows them side by side, then whatever the adjudicator ruled. Each review between revision rounds is one reader's report, not a pair.

The first readings read the entries as they stood after the second revision round, not as they stand now. The entry's words quoted in section 3 are the final words; a reading's words may answer an earlier wording. The revision logs of section 4 show what moved.

Of the 112 files the project lists as landed, 53 were opened and quoted, 48 were opened and checked but not quoted, and 11 were not opened (closing messages, named by the listed digest only). Section 7 lists every one. The project's records also name 26 further outputs that have no durable file at any named place, and 6 completion records that list no digest; those outputs could not be read by anyone and are not represented here beyond the list in section 7.

## 3. The disputes

A passage appears here if any first reading challenged its entry, if the first readings differed, if a review recorded a finding or left a seam unaccepted on it, if an adjudication named it, or if an audit recorded a finding on it or did not confirm it. 67 of the 70 units appear. They are ordered by the highest severity any reader assigned, then by their place in the book. Severity is the readers' own grade.

The readers used different words for severity. For ordering only, this record ranks them as follows: blocker, major and high rank as high; medium as medium; minor and low as low; note, or no grade, last. The reader's own word is printed with each finding.

By highest severity: high, 9; medium, 16; low, 39; note or none recorded, 3.

### 1 Corinthians 7:32–35 — highest severity: high

The stated reason for the boundary, as the entry now reads:

Close at 7:35/7:36: 7:35 states the purpose of the counsel, 7:35 (RP) (Τοῦτο δὲ πρὸς τὸ ὑμῶν αὐτῶν συμφέρον λέγω), and 7:36 begins a conditional case, 7:36 (RP) (Εἰ δέ τις).

### 1 Corinthians 9:1–14 — highest severity: high

The unit as it stands: language Greek; type example; confidence medium-low.

The stated reason for the boundary, as the entry now reads:

> The entry opens at 9:1 with asyndetic questions, Οὐκ εἰμὶ ἀπόστολος (9:1 (RP)) and Οὐκ εἰμὶ ἐλεύθερος (9:1 (RP)), which turn from the idol-food case to Paul's own rights. The freedom question answers the right named at 8:9, ἡ ἐξουσία ὑμῶν αὕτη (8:9 (RP)), so 9:1 sits inside the idol-food argument as an example and not as a new subject. Behind the seam the resolve Διόπερ (8:13 (RP)) closes the case of the weak conscience. SBLGNT reads the two questions in the other order, with Οὐκ εἰμὶ ⸂ἐλεύθερος (9:1 (SBLGNT)) leading. The defence word Ἡ ἐμὴ ἀπολογία (9:3 (RP)) stands at 9:3 (counted, a counting script: 1 verse book-wide in RP and SBLGNT). The appeal Οὐκ οἴδατε (9:13 (RP)) at 9:13 adds a temple warrant inside the entry and does not open a new move. The entry closes at 9:14 on the command of the Lord, Οὕτως καὶ ὁ κύριος διέταξεν (9:14 (RP)), which ends the run of warrants for the right to support. Across the seam Ἐγὼ δὲ οὐδενὶ ἐχρησάμην τούτων (9:15 (RP)) turns to Paul's non-use of the right, which Ἀλλ’ οὐκ ἐχρησάμεθα τῇ ἐξουσίᾳ ταύτῃ (9:12 (RP)) at 9:12 previews. The medium-low grade records doubt over the typing and, under one reading, over the seams: the turns at 9:1 and 9:15 are worded, but chapter 9 also reads as a defence of apostolic rights, a reading that would retype the entry and could set a seam at 9:2/9:3 or join the entry to 9:15-9:23.

The strongest alternative the entry considered and declined:

> The defence reading takes 9:3, τοῖς ἐμὲ ἀνακρίνουσιν αὕτη ἐστίν (9:3 (RP)), as a heading over a separate apologia, which would set a seam at 9:2/9:3 or treat chapter 9 as one unit of defence apart from the idol-food argument. Rejected for the seam: the questions of 9:1 resume in 9:4-9:6 after the statements of 9:2-9:3, and the apostle word of 9:1-9:2 returns at 9:5, and whether 9:3 points back to 9:1-9:2 or forward to 9:4 stays open. A merge of 9:1-9:23 is also weighed and turned down, since 9:15 shifts from the right to its renunciation.

> keep

The close at 9:14/9:15 is weighed from both sides: Οὕτως καὶ ὁ κύριος διέταξεν (9:14 (RP)) caps the warrants, and Ἐγὼ δὲ οὐδενὶ ἐχρησάμην τούτων (9:15 (RP)) turns to non-use. The defence reading is reported as a rival, using Ἡ ἐμὴ ἀπολογία (9:3 (RP)), as a section of the reading plan asks, and is not preferred. The appeal Οὐκ οἴδατε (9:13 (RP)) is correctly kept as texture inside the entry. example is within a section of the reading plan vocabulary for chapter 9.

### 1 Corinthians 9:24–27 — highest severity: high

The unit as it stands: language Greek; type example; confidence medium-low.

The stated reason for the boundary, as the entry now reads:

> The entry opens at 9:24 with the appeal Οὐκ οἴδατε (9:24 (RP)) and the direct charge Οὕτως τρέχετε (9:24 (RP)), which move from Paul's conduct to the readers. The same appeal stands at 9:13 as well (counted, a counting script: 2 RP verses in 1 Corinthians 8:1-11:1), so here it marks a turn together with the change of addressee. Behind the seam the summary Τοῦτο δὲ ποιῶ διὰ τὸ εὐαγγέλιον (9:23 (RP)) closes the renunciation. The entry closes at 9:27 on the fear of disqualification, ἀδόκιμος γένωμαι (9:27 (RP)). Across the seam Οὐ θέλω δὲ ὑμᾶς ἀγνοεῖν, ἀδελφοί (10:1 (RP)) opens the Israel example with a disclosure formula and vocative. SBLGNT reads ⸀γὰρ (10:1 (SBLGNT)) at that point, which ties 10:1 back to the self-discipline warning. Typing as example is held open against an exhortation reading. Here the seam itself is in doubt as well as the typing, because merges across 9:23/9:24 and across 9:27/10:1 are both weighed against it.

The strongest alternative the entry considered and declined:

> The leading alternative attaches 9:24-9:27 to 10:1-10:13 as one warning, on a pattern that crosses the seam, a whole company set against part of it: πάντες μὲν τρέχουσιν, εἷς δὲ λαμβάνει (9:24 (RP)) sets the whole field of runners against the one who wins, and the repeated πάντες (10:1 (RP)) of 10:1-10:4 meets Ἀλλ’ οὐκ ἐν τοῖς πλείοσιν (10:5 (RP)). The merge is turned down because ὑμᾶς ἀγνοεῖν, ἀδελφοί (10:1 (RP)) stands in both witnesses, so the disclosure formula restarts the address whichever connective is read. A merge back into 9:15-9:23 is also weighed and turned down because the addressee changes at 9:24.

**Evidence on each side.**

Reading A: verdict challenge; severity low; graded by the reader as interpretive; its proposed course:

> reword the strongest alternative considered

Its claim:

### 1 Corinthians 1:26–31 — highest severity: medium

The unit as it stands: language Greek; type argument step; confidence medium.

The stated reason for the boundary, as the entry now reads:

> The argument shifts from third-person thesis to the second person at 1:26 (RP) Βλέπετε γὰρ τὴν κλῆσιν ὑμῶν, ἀδελφοί, an imperative on the present drafter reading; γὰρ links back, and the warrant is the turn to the Corinthians' own calling: few of them wise, powerful or well-born, and God choosing the foolish, weak and despised. Purpose clauses lead to Christ made wisdom, righteousness, sanctification and redemption. The stretch closes on the cited boast 1:31 (RP) Ὁ καυχώμενος, ἐν κυρίῳ καυχάσθω. Robinson-Pierpont prints a pilcrow at 1:26, where this step begins. At 2:1 a past-tense narrative of arrival with vocative begins, so the seam falls after 1:31.

The strongest alternative the entry considered and declined:

> Join 1:26-1:31 to 1:18-1:25 as one argument about divine wisdom and human folly. Rejected because the second-person turn at 1:26, though 1:26 (RP) γὰρ links back, moves from general thesis to the readers' social makeup, and the citation at 1:31 gives the stretch its own end.

**Evidence on each side.**

Reading A: verdict challenge; severity low; graded by the reader as interpretive; its proposed course:

> reword the strongest alternative considered; confidence stands

### 1 Corinthians 11:2–12 — highest severity: medium

The unit as it stands: language Greek; type argument step; confidence medium-low.

The stated reason for the boundary, as the entry now reads:

Across the seam, 11:2 (RP) Ἐπαινῶ δὲ ὑμᾶς, opens a statement of praise whose object is the delivered traditions (11:2 (RP) καθὼς παρέδωκα ὑμῖν τὰς παραδόσεις); the praise verb returns negated at 11:17 (RP) οὐκ ἐπαινῶ, so the pair brackets the material before the meal discussion (read from the source: device ids epaino and the formula “οὐκ ἐπαινῶ 'I do not praise'” at 11:2, 11:17 and 11:22). No entry opens at 11:3: 11:3 (RP) Θέλω δὲ ὑμᾶς εἰδέναι, states the thesis that the praise of 11:2 leads into, and its connective ties it back to 11:2 (interpretive). Close at 11:12, weighed from both sides. Inside, 11:12 (RP) τὰ δὲ πάντα ἐκ τοῦ θεοῦ. ends the reciprocity qualification that 11:11 (RP) Πλὴν introduced, which answers the argument from the creation account in 11:7-11:10 (interpretive). Beyond, 11:13 (RP) Ἐν ὑμῖν αὐτοῖς κρίνατε· begins asyndetically and turns from argument to an appeal to the hearers' own judgment, followed by the argument from nature and the appeal to custom (interpretive). This close seam rests on the text alone: there is no RP pilcrow at 11:13, and no device from the device listing stands there (read from the source), so the evidence for it is one-sided and confidence stays medium-low. A second ground for medium-low is specific to this entry: the merge of 11:2-11:12 with 11:13-11:16 remains live, so the close is held against an open rival (interpretive).

The appeal in 11:13-11:16 argues for the same practice as 11:4-11:10, and a reader who weighs the shared subject over the change in kind of warrant would merge the two; that merge is the live rival and is left as a question for final review. In its favour, 11:13 (RP) ἀκατακάλυπτον returns to the adjective of 11:5 (RP) ἀκατακαλύπτῳ across the proposed seam (interpretive). The split is kept because the connective-free opening of 11:13, with its second-person address at 11:13 (RP) ὑμῖν, changes the mode of argument from the creation account to the hearers' judgment, nature and custom (interpretive). A second candidate, a seam at 11:10/11:11, where 11:11 (RP) Πλὴν turns the argument, is rejected: 11:11-11:12 qualifies 11:7-11:10, and since 11:12 (RP) Ὥσπερ γὰρ grounds 11:11 inside that pair, the tie back rests on 11:11 (RP) Πλὴν and on the echoes of 11:8-11:9 at 11:12 (11:12 (RP) ἐκ τοῦ ἀνδρός and 11:12 (RP) διὰ τῆς γυναικός), so the pair completes that argument rather than opening a move (interpretive).

### 1 Corinthians 11:13–16 — highest severity: medium

The unit as it stands: language Greek; type appeal; confidence medium-low.

The stated reason for the boundary, as the entry now reads:

> Onset at 11:13, weighed from both sides. Behind the seam, 11:12 (RP) τὰ δὲ πάντα ἐκ τοῦ θεοῦ. closes the qualification that began at 11:11 by grounding man and woman in God (interpretive). Across it, 11:13 (RP) Ἐν ὑμῖν αὐτοῖς κρίνατε· opens asyndetically and asks the hearers to judge (11:13 (WEB) “Judge for yourselves.”), with the person shifting to the second-person 11:13 (RP) ὑμῖν after the third-person statements of 11:4-11:12 (interpretive), and the verses after it argue from what nature teaches (11:14 (WEB) “Doesn’t even nature itself teach you”) rather than from the creation account. No device from the device listing and no RP pilcrow stands at 11:13 (read from the source), so the onset rests on the turn in argument alone, which keeps confidence medium-low. Close at 11:16, weighed from both sides. Inside, 11:16 (RP) ἡμεῖς τοιαύτην συνήθειαν οὐκ ἔχομεν, appeals to a shared custom, widened by 11:16 (RP) οὐδὲ αἱ ἐκκλησίαι τοῦ θεοῦ., and sets aside further dispute; it ends the discussion of covering. Beyond, 11:17 (RP) Τοῦτο δὲ παραγγέλλων οὐκ ἐπαινῶ, turns to the assemblies at their meals and negates the praise of 11:2 (read from the source: device ids epaino and the formula “οὐκ ἐπαινῶ 'I do not praise'” at 11:2 and 11:17). This close seam is also the seam between the head-covering material and the meal material.

The strongest alternative the entry considered and declined:

> Joining 11:13-11:16 to the material from 11:2, as one entry for 11:2-11:16: the appeal pursues the same practice, and a reader who weighs the shared subject over the change in mode would merge it. It is not taken because the onset at 11:13 changes the kind of warrant offered, from the creation account to the hearers' judgment, nature and custom; the merge is recorded as the live rival and is a question for final review. A further option, a separate unit for 11:16 as the closing appeal to custom, is rejected because 11:16 (RP) Εἰ δέ τις δοκεῖ, a conditional, answers the argument of 11:13-11:15 and is too slight to stand as a move by itself (interpretive).

### 1 Corinthians 12:12–26 — highest severity: medium

The unit as it stands: language Greek; type argument step; confidence medium.

The stated reason for the boundary, as the entry now reads:

> Onset at 12:12 with Καθάπερ γὰρ (12:12 (RP)), where body and member vocabulary enters chapter 12 (read from the source from both witnesses at 12:1-12:12). The causal γὰρ (12:12 (RP)) ties the verse back across the seam, and ἐν ἑνὶ πνεύματι (12:13 (RP)) at 12:13 keeps the Spirit of 12:11 in view. The image is worked out through shared baptism at 12:13 and the speeches of foot, ear, eye and head at 12:15-12:21. At 12:21 the eye cannot say Χρείαν σου οὐκ ἔχω (12:21 (RP)) to the hand. The close comes at 12:26 with Καὶ εἴτε πάσχει ἓν μέλος, συμπάσχει πάντα τὰ μέλη (12:26 (RP)), a two-sided conclusion of shared suffering and shared honour. At 12:27 Ὑμεῖς δέ ἐστε σῶμα χριστοῦ (12:27 (RP)) turns from the analogy to its application.

The strongest alternative the entry considered and declined:

> A cut at 12:14 is conceivable, yet Καὶ γὰρ τὸ σῶμα (12:14 (RP)) restates 12:13 with a parallel kai gar (transliterated gloss) and keeps the same image in play. A cut at 12:21 is weaker too, since the head and feet extend speeches already running. At fifteen verses the unit stays within the size range.

**Evidence on each side.**

Reading A: verdict support; severity none given; graded by the reader as counted; its proposed course:

> keep

Its claim:

> 12:12-12:26 is one sustained body analogy: thesis at 12:12-13, imagined speeches of members at 12:15-16 and 12:21 (12:21 holds both the eye's and the head's speech), concluding in the shared suffering and honour of 12:26. The close is two-sided: 12:27 turns the image onto the addressees. The disclosed SBL sites (12:12, 12:13, 12:18, 12:20, 12:24-26) agree with pmarks; the WEB paragraph at 12:14 is internal and rightly not used.

### 1 Corinthians 13:8–13 — highest severity: medium

The unit as it stands: language Greek; type inset; confidence medium.

The stated reason for the boundary, as the entry now reads:

> Onset at 13:8 with Ἡ ἀγάπη οὐδέποτε ἐκπίπτει (13:8 (RP)), setting the permanence of love against prophecies, tongues and knowledge, which pass. The partial-versus-complete contrast runs through 13:9-13:12 and turns on ὅταν δὲ ἔλθῃ τὸ τέλειον (13:10 (RP)) at 13:10. The mirror image at 13:12 follows: Βλέπομεν γὰρ ἄρτι δι’ ἐσόπτρου ἐν αἰνίγματι (13:12 (RP)). The close at 13:13 is the triad Νυνὶ δὲ μένει πίστις, ἐλπίς, ἀγάπη (13:13 (RP)), crowned by love. At 14:1 Διώκετε τὴν ἀγάπην (14:1 (RP)) resumes exhortation on the basis of the inset.

The strongest alternative the entry considered and declined:

> Ending the inset at 13:12 and joining 13:13 to 14:1 would treat the triad as a bridge to the pursuit of love. The triad answers the partial-versus-complete contrast and names love as greater, so it closes the inset, and 14:1 restarts with the verse-initial, connective-free Διώκετε (14:1 (RP)).

**Evidence on each side.**

> keep

Its claim:

> 13:8-13:13 holds: permanence of love set against ceasing gifts, the partial-versus-complete contrast through 13:9-12, closed by the triad Νυνὶ δὲ μένει πίστις, ἐλπίς, ἀγάπη at 13:13. The close is two-sided: 14:1 Διώκετε τὴν ἀγάπην resumes exhortation and begins the zeloute bracket's second member. The SBL sites 13:8-13:11 match pmarks; the WEB quote at 13:10 is exact; the divided readings of to teleion are named neutrally and the seam is kept independent of them, as the reading plan requires.

### 1 Corinthians 14:34–36 — highest severity: medium

The unit as it stands: language Greek; type exhortation; confidence low.

The stated reason for the boundary, as the entry now reads:

> Onset at 14:34 with Αἱ γυναῖκες ὑμῶν ἐν ταῖς ἐκκλησίαις (14:34 (RP)): a new subject enters without a connective after the ground stated in 14:33. At 14:34 SBL reads Αἱ ⸀γυναῖκες ἐν ταῖς ἐκκλησίαις (14:34 (SBLGNT)), omitting the possessive. The instruction continues in 14:35 with Εἰ δέ τι μαθεῖν θέλουσιν (14:35 (RP)). At 14:36 the double question Ἢ ἀφ’ ὑμῶν ὁ λόγος τοῦ θεοῦ ἐξῆλθεν (14:36 (RP)) challenges the addressees and is attached backward here, as a retort that closes the instruction; this attachment is interpretive. Close after 14:36: 14:37 opens a conditional with Εἴ τις δοκεῖ προφήτης εἶναι (14:37 (RP)).

The strongest alternative the entry considered and declined:

> Attaching 14:36 forward to the conditional of 14:37 is the rival, reading the questions as the opening of a closing challenge to self-styled prophets. The questions address the assembly as a whole with second-person plurals, while 14:37 narrows to anyone who thinks himself a prophet, so 14:36 is kept with the instruction as a soft judgment left open, and the recurrence of ταῖς ἐκκλησίαις (14:33 (RP)) and ταῖς ἐκκλησίαις (14:34 (RP)) on either side of the onset is weighed as a tie that pulls toward 14:33 but does not displace the new subject at 14:34 (interpretive).

**Evidence on each side.**

Reading A: verdict challenge; severity medium; graded by the reader as read from the source; its proposed course:

> reword the formula notes

Its claim:

> The divided-reading sentence states that 14:34-14:35 stands in divided readings in debates over ordination without a evidence grade label. The reading plan's only source for that context is reported (hardness batch 03). The sentence also does not name the readings or their holders, which a section of the reading plan and a section of the reading plan close check (item 9) require. Secondary finding: the closing claim that both wordings are given wherever the two editions differ in 14:34-14:35 overclaims. At least two RP/SBL differences without an SBL critical sign are not given: the noun at 14:35 (RP plural, SBL singular) and the adversative at 14:34 (RP elided, SBL full). The seam, the interpretive backward attachment of 14:36 and low confidence stand.

The two sides as it set them out:

> For the entry: it prefers no reading, keeps the debate out of the seam reasoning, labels the transposition reported, and discloses every SBL-signed site in 14:34-14:35. Against: an unlabelled report reads as an assertion (an owner directive provenance evidence grades), the reading plan requires readings and holders to be named, and 'both wordings are given' is a universal claim that the unsigned differences falsify. The pinned inputs do not name the readings or their holders, so the minimum available fix is the reported label with its source, plus narrowing the both-wordings claim to the signed sites.

The evidence it cited:

> 1 Corinthians 14:34–36 the formula notes: 'The instruction at 14:34-14:35 stands in divided readings in debates over ordination; no preference between those readings is taken here.'

### 1 Corinthians 1:1–3 — highest severity: low

The prescript ends with the greeting 1:3 (RP) χάρις ὑμῖν καὶ εἰρήνη. At 1:4 the speech act changes from salutation to thanks, so the seam falls after 1:3; the RP pilcrow at 1:4 stands at that seam, though the cut rests on the connective-free onset of the letter's finite verbs, the prescript having no finite verb in either witness, the present drafter reading.

Set aside because prescript and thanksgiving are two distinct letter forms, the prescript carrying no finite verb in either witness and the thanksgiving opening on one, and keeping them apart lets the address and the thanks be weighed on their own terms.

> The formula 3:16 (RP) Οὐκ οἴδατε ὅτι ναὸς θεοῦ ἐστε addresses the readers as God's temple indwelt by the Spirit. Then 3:17 adds the warning that God will destroy whoever destroys that temple, ending 3:17 (RP) οἵτινές ἐστε ὑμεῖς. The two verses form a compact unit framed by the temple word. An RP pilcrow stands at 3:16, this unit's opening verse, and another at 3:18, the opening verse of the next paragraph, which places it at the seam after this short unit, where a prohibition against self-deception begins; the conditional series running from 3:12 to 3:18 crosses both seams, with 3:15 (RP) Εἴ τινος before this unit, 3:17 (RP) Εἴ τις inside it and 3:18 (RP) εἴ τις after it. Confidence is moderate-to-low: a two-verse unit is short, and the verses can be read as the end of 3:10-3:15 or the head of 3:18-3:23.

Paul counts human judgment, even his own self-examination, as of little weight; the Lord is the judge. The step ends with the inference 4:5 (RP) Ὥστε μὴ πρὸ καιροῦ τι κρίνετε and the promise of praise from God. At 4:6 a vocative and a first-person statement about applying these things to himself and Apollos open a new step, and the RP pilcrow at 4:6 falls at that turn.

### 1 Corinthians 4:14–21 — highest severity: low

The unit as it stands: language Greek; type exhortation; confidence medium.

The stated reason for the boundary, as the entry now reads:

> With 4:14 (RP) Οὐκ ἐντρέπων ὑμᾶς γράφω ταῦτα Paul shifts to paternal admonition: many guardians, one father who begot them through the gospel. The appeal 4:16 (RP) Παρακαλῶ οὖν ὑμᾶς, μιμηταί μου γίνεσθε calls for imitation. The sending of Timothy follows in 4:17 (RP) Διὰ τοῦτο ἔπεμψα ὑμῖν Τιμόθεον. Those who are puffed up are warned of his coming, and the stretch ends on the question 4:21 (RP) Τί θέλετε, rod or love. The RP pilcrow at 4:14 coincides with this onset. A new matter opens with 5:1 (RP) Ὅλως ἀκούεται ἐν ὑμῖν πορνεία, so the seam falls after 4:21.

The strongest alternative the entry considered and declined:

> Treat 4:17-4:21 as its own travel notice, opening with the sending of Timothy and the planned visit. Rejected: the sending serves the call to imitation in 4:16, and the warning of 4:18-4:21 continues the father's address, so the travel note is embedded in the admonition.

**Evidence on each side.**

Reading A: verdict support; severity none given; graded by the reader as read from the source; its proposed course:

> keep

Its claim:

> reading plan item a numbered rule allows 4:14-4:21 as one entry. ### 1 Corinthians 6:1–8 — highest severity: low

The unit as it stands: language Greek; type argument step; confidence medium-low.

The stated reason for the boundary, as the entry now reads:

> Onset at 6:1 is the parent seam: behind it 5:13 closes the expulsion ruling with a citation-imperative, an RP future that the mood term interprets; across it 6:1 (RP) (Τολμᾷ τις ὑμῶν) opens a new complaint. Inside the span, question formulas at 6:2 and 6:3 drive the rebuke, 6:3 (RP) (Οὐκ οἴδατε ὅτι ἀγγέλους κρινοῦμεν). Close at 6:8/6:9 is held with less confidence: 6:8 ends on the charge 6:8 (RP) (Ἀλλὰ ὑμεῖς ἀδικεῖτε), and 6:9 opens with a disjunctive question formula whose key word picks up that charge, 6:9 (RP) (Ἢ οὐκ οἴδατε ὅτι ἄδικοι). The lexical link pulls across the seam, so the close has one-sided support from the formula.

The strongest alternative the entry considered and declined:

> Running 6:1-6:11 as one move, since 6:8 accuses the readers of wrongdoing and 6:9 answers with the fate of the wrongdoers. The wrongdoing stem runs through that span: 6:1 (RP) (ἀδίκων) naming the outside court, 6:7 (RP) (ἀδικεῖσθε) on suffering wrong, 6:8 (RP) (ἀδικεῖτε) on doing it, and 6:9 (RP) (ἄδικοι) on those shut out of the kingdom. ### 1 Corinthians 10:23–30 — highest severity: low

### 1 Corinthians 10:31–11:1 — highest severity: low

Behind the seam the dinner case ends with the paired questions of 10:29b-10:30, the second being τί βλασφημοῦμαι (10:30 (RP)), and εὐχαριστῶ (10:30 (RP)) there stays texture. The charge widens at 10:32 to Jews, Greeks and τῇ ἐκκλησίᾳ τοῦ θεοῦ (10:32 (RP)), and 10:33 grounds it in Paul's own practice, καθὼς κἀγὼ (10:33 (RP)) and μὴ ζητῶν τὸ ἐμαυτοῦ συμφέρον (10:33 (RP)). Through the span and past it, the gathering verb (the stem of 11:17 (RP) συνέρχεσθε.) stands in 5 verses of chapter 11 in RP, at 11:17, 11:18, 11:20, 11:33 and 11:34 (counted, skeleton match). Close at 11:22, weighed from both sides. The phrase is a refrain, not a frame, because it sounds inside the unit as well: κατὰ τὸ αὐτὸ πνεῦμα (12:8 (RP)) and ἐν τῷ αὐτῷ πνεύματι (12:9 (RP)), the latter twice in RP, where the second instance in SBL is ἐν τῷ ⸀ἑνὶ πνεύματι (12:9 (SBLGNT)). The close rests on πάντα δὲ ταῦτα (12:11 (RP)), which gathers the list, and on the change of image at 12:12. Close after 12:11: 12:12 opens the body analogy with Καθάπερ γὰρ (12:12 (RP)).

The strongest alternative the entry considered and declined:

> A cut between 12:7 and 12:8 would separate the principle of manifestation from the list at 12:8-12:10 that unpacks it, so the list stays with its principle. Joining 12:4-12:26 into one unit would run past the gathering of the list at 12:11 and the change of image at 12:12.

> keep

### 1 Corinthians 13:1–3 — highest severity: low

### 1 Corinthians 13:4–7 — highest severity: low

The unit as it stands: language Greek; type inset; confidence medium.

The stated reason for the boundary, as the entry now reads:

> Onset at 13:4, where Ἡ ἀγάπη μακροθυμεῖ (13:4 (RP)) begins a chain of predicates with love as repeated subject. At 13:4-13:6 the verbs run two positive, then eight negated, then one positive (read from the source from the negators in both witnesses). The chain reaches a fourfold cadence at 13:7, where πάντα στέγει (13:7 (RP)) heads four clauses built on panta (transliterated gloss). At 13:8 Ἡ ἀγάπη οὐδέποτε ἐκπίπτει (13:8 (RP)) restates love as subject and turns to permanence set against gifts that cease.

The strongest alternative the entry considered and declined:

> 13:8a could close the chain, since its opening words repeat the subject of 13:4 and complete the description; a unit of 13:4-13:8 was weighed. Units are verse-granular and the remainder of 13:8 belongs with the contrast of passing gifts, so 13:8 opens the following unit.

> reword the evidence list

### 1 Corinthians 14:1–5 — highest severity: low

The unit sets prophecy above uninterpreted tongues by the criterion of building up the gathering, and 14:5 closes it with ἵνα ἡ ἐκκλησία οἰκοδομὴν λάβῃ (14:5 (RP)). At 14:6 Νυνὶ δέ, ἀδελφοί (14:6 (RP)) begins a hypothetical visit and a run of analogies.

### 1 Corinthians 14:6–12 — highest severity: low

The unit as it stands: language Greek; type argument step; confidence medium-low.

The stated reason for the boundary, as the entry now reads:

> Onset at 14:6 with Νυνὶ δέ, ἀδελφοί (14:6 (RP)), a hypothetical visit in tongues that would bring the hearers no profit. Analogies from musical instruments and foreign speech follow at 14:7-14:11. The application at 14:9 reads Οὕτως καὶ ὑμεῖς (14:9 (RP)). The same words return at 14:12 as the unit closes: Οὕτως καὶ ὑμεῖς (14:12 (RP)). The twice-applied words are a refrain and not a frame, since the analogies run on past 14:9 through 14:11, and 14:12 ends on edifying the gathering. At 14:13 the inferential connective Διόπερ (14:13 (RP)) draws a practical consequence.

The strongest alternative the entry considered and declined:

> The inferential connective at 14:13 could be read as concluding 14:6-14:12, which would place the seam after 14:13. It is read here as opening the prescription that 14:13-14:19 develops, but the seam is soft and the attachment of 14:13 remains open.

The formula at 14:9 applies the instrument analogies of 14:7-14:8, and the same formula at 14:12 applies the speech analogy of 14:10-14:11. Because 14:9 stands inside the analogy run, the repetition cannot bracket 14:7-14:11; it is a twice-applied refrain whose second occurrence closes the unit. The seam after 14:12 and medium-low confidence stand.

The two sides as it set them out:

Against the wording: a frame stands before and after the framed material, while 14:9 stands between the two analogy pairs; calling it a frame misdescribes the device that the close relies on.

### 1 Corinthians 14:37–40 — highest severity: low

The unit as it stands: language Greek; type exhortation; confidence medium.

The stated reason for the boundary, as the entry now reads:

> Onset at 14:37 with Εἴ τις δοκεῖ προφήτης εἶναι (14:37 (RP)). The conditional addresses anyone claiming prophetic or spiritual standing, who must acknowledge ἃ γράφω ὑμῖν (14:37 (RP)) as a command of the Lord. The sanction follows at 14:38 with Εἰ δέ τις ἀγνοεῖ (14:38 (RP)). At 14:39 Ὥστε, ἀδελφοί, ζηλοῦτε τὸ προφητεύειν (14:39 (RP)) brings back the verb seen at 12:31 and 14:1 and gathers up the chapter's comparison of prophecy and tongues; the directive force of its two verbs is a matter of interpretation and not of measurement (interpretive), and so taken, prophecy is to be sought eagerly, and speaking in tongues is not to be hindered. Then 14:40 closes with πάντα εὐσχημόνως καὶ κατὰ τάξιν γινέσθω (14:40 (RP)). At 15:1 Γνωρίζω δὲ ὑμῖν, ἀδελφοί (15:1 (RP)) introduces the gospel tradition with a disclosure formula.

The strongest alternative the entry considered and declined:

> A cut at 14:39 is the rival. There hoste with the vocative opens a summary, but cutting would leave 14:37-14:38 as a two-verse unit. The summary is phrased as the closing exhortation of this conditional section, and the hoste-plus-vocative opening is a known over-split temptation that is not followed.

**Evidence on each side.**

Reading A: verdict support; severity none given; graded by the reader as read from the source; its proposed course:

> keep

Its claim:

> The unit 14:37-14:40 is a well-formed closing section of the chapter. Its onset is a conditional addressed to self-styled prophets, which an RP pilcrow at 14:37 informs but does not drive. Its close is the order maxim at 14:40, which reading plan a numbered rule lists as a measured closer. The rejected cut at the hoste-plus-vocative summary of 14:39 is correctly refused as an over-split that would leave a two-verse unit. The SBL variant sites at 14:37-14:40 are all disclosed and quoted exactly, and medium confidence stands. ### 1 Corinthians 7:25–31 — highest severity: note or none recorded

The unit as it stands: language Greek; type topic response; confidence medium-low.

The stated reason for the boundary, as the entry now reads:

> Onset at 7:25 is the parent seam: behind it 7:24 closes the calling refrain. Across it the topic marker opens the question of virgins, 7:25 (RP) (Περὶ δὲ τῶν παρθένων). RP pilcrow at 7:25 coincides with this onset (read from the source). Inside the span a vocative formula at 7:29 restates the time frame, 7:29 (RP) (Τοῦτο δέ φημι, ἀδελφοί). Close at 7:31/7:32: 7:31 ends on a maxim about the world's form, 7:31 (RP) (παράγει γὰρ τὸ σχῆμα τοῦ κόσμου τούτου), and 7:32 begins with a wish, 7:32 (RP) (Θέλω δὲ ὑμᾶς ἀμερίμνους εἶναι). The close is held at reduced confidence because the material on either side shares its theme.

The strongest alternative the entry considered and declined:

> A seam at 7:28/7:29, opening a unit at the vocative formula 7:29 (RP) (Τοῦτο δέ φημι, ἀδελφοί). Declined at reduced confidence because 7:29-7:31 ground the counsel of 7:26-7:28 in the shortened time and do not change the topic; the alternative remains open.

**Evidence on each side.**

Reading A: verdict support; severity none given; graded by the reader as read from the source (device inventory: the formula “περὶ δέ 'now concerning', any position” 7:25; the formula “τοῦτο δέ φημι 'this I say'” 7:29 family other marker; adelphoi 7:29; the formula “θέλω δὲ (πάντας) ὑμᾶς + any infinitive: a wider wish form, NOT counted in the disclosure family” 7:32, not counted in the disclosure family); its proposed course:

> no change

Its claim:

> Extent 7:25-7:31 is supported: a numbered rule is met at 7:25 and topic response is the correct type; the formula “τοῦτο δέ φημι 'this I say'” rival at 7:29 is declined consistently with a section of the reading plan texture; the close sets the 7:31 gar maxim against the 7:32 wider wish form, which the entry correctly does not count as a disclosure. medium-low is not stronger than the evidence. The entry's phrase 'vocative formula' is loose (the inventory files the formula “τοῦτο δέ φημι 'this I say'” under other marker), but no seam is claimed from it, so it is not a defect.

The two sides as it set them out:

> Onset 7:25: back side 7:24 refrain close; front side 7:25 (RP) «Περὶ δὲ τῶν παρθένων» with 7:25 (RP) «γνώμην δὲ δίδωμι». Close 7:31/7:32: back side 7:31 grounds the counsel in a gar maxim; front side 7:32 opens the wider wish form and a new contrast of anxieties. Both sides assessed by the entry.

The evidence it cited:

> 7:25 (RP) «Περὶ δὲ τῶν παρθένων»

Limit. The fourth and fifth revision rounds left no revision log in the list of landed files, so the words of their changes are not printed here. What is known of them is what the readers who followed them counted. The review of the fourth revision counted 13 entries and 17 elements changed. The second audit counted 21 entries and 26 elements changed by the fifth revision and 49 entries byte-identical.

## 5. The reviews, the adjudication and the audit

Each review between revision rounds was one reader's report. Each is summarised below, with its findings that bear on many passages or on the apparatus rather than on one passage. Its findings on one to 3 passages stand under those passages in section 3.

### The review of the first draft

Its verdict on readiness: fit with changes. New findings: 13 (major, 4; minor, 4; note, 5). Earlier findings it re-examined: 0 (none). Conditions: 0 (none).

### The review of the fourth revision

Its verdict on readiness: fit with changes. New findings: 3 (blocker, 2; note, 1). Earlier findings it re-examined: 0 (none). Conditions: 7 (met, 6; not met, 1).

A finding (severity note) on a working file; a working file:

> The pinned run, COPY (with its settings), cannot see a 7-gram shared by fewer than ten entries, so the list's passing and offending [] say nothing about condition 3's second clause, and the list records no check of it.

> Pinned run: passing, offending [], on both files. The clone run shows two-entry sharing under the same arguments: 269 offending grams on revision round 3, 255 on revision round 4, net 14 fewer, with six of the 255 new. The clone counts are a measuring device, not the book's checkpoint result.

> A later checkpoint that keeps this clause should name a run able to answer it.

A condition it held not met:

> a working file on the revision round 4 applied entries: hard status passing, no dead member, the repeated-phrase check passing at checkpoint 10 with no new 7-gram shared across entries from revision round 4 text, register and universals passing.

> Every named part is passing except one clause. set of automated checks: hard status passing, dead members [], the repeated-phrase check, register and universals passing. checkpoint 10: passing, offending []. The clause 'no new 7-gram shared across entries from revision round 4 text' fails: six 7-grams from revision round 4 text are newly shared by two entries, see a numbered review finding and a numbered review finding. Read literally, the clause adds something only below ten entries, because a gram reaching ten entries already fails checkpoint 10. If the adjudicator meant the clause to mean checkpoint 10 only, this condition is met; that reading is mine to report and not mine to choose.

What it said must change:

> Condition 3: the ordered 1 Corinthians 10:1–13 the formula notes text newly shares three 7-grams with 1 Corinthians 9:24–27. Either order an amended exact span or state that the clause reaches only checkpoint 10. The bytes are ordered, so no author and no editor may reword them.

> Condition 3: the ordered the stated reason for the boundary text in 1 Corinthians 16:1–4 and 1 Corinthians 16:12 newly shares three 7-grams. Either order an amended exact span in one entry, 1 Corinthians 16:12 being the tested candidate, or state that the clause reaches only checkpoint 10.

Its test of a stated reason for not using a drafter:

> every author order carries exact spans, so an author would have had nothing to write

> holds

> All 10 author orders read whole. Each carries exact spans or a whole append text. None adds a further edit, a condition beyond the byte restrictions, a duty to choose words or a duty to re-check. The restrictions were measured and hold.

### Adjudications

Adjudications that concern a class of entries or more than 3 passages, and so are not placed under a single passage in section 3 (43):

- Round 1, bearing on 0 named passages (ratify with changes):

> Every finding entry is routed to exactly one revision reading keyed by its drafter part prefix: drafting part 2, drafting part 3, drafting part 4, drafting part 5, drafting part 6. drafting part 1 receives no revision reading (zero review stage 1 findings, zero a standing order baseline hits, zero rule-14 failures). Entries added by a standing order classification route to the owning part of their the entry number prefix.

> One owner per entry prevents two authors editing one entry; the part prefix is the existing ownership key in a working file.

- Round 1, bearing on 5 named passages (ratify with changes):

> drafting part 5 entries 1 Corinthians 12:1–3, 1 Corinthians 12:4–11, 1 Corinthians 12:12–26, 1 Corinthians 14:13–19, 1 Corinthians 14:20–25, 1 Corinthians 14:26–33, 1 Corinthians 14:34–36, 1 Corinthians 14:37–40 remove every WEB-paragraph mention used as layout evidence: from the stated reason for the boundary and the strongest alternative considered delete the WEB-paragraph clause; from the observed signals delete every item naming a WEB paragraph; from the evidence list delete every web: ref naming a WEB paragraph or tagged the mark disclosure. An RP pilcrow may remain at most once per entry, as one the observed signals item naming only its location. The the repeated-phrase check gram is cured by rewording, never by a tool exclusion.

> a section of the reading plan rule 4 bars WEB paragraphs from driving or corroborating a boundary; rule 5 lets an RP pilcrow inform only. The single offending the repeated-phrase check gram 'rp pilcrow at and web paragraph at' is formed by adjacent the observed signals items, e.g. 1 Corinthians 12:4–11 ['RP pilcrow at 12:4 and 12:12', 'WEB paragraph at 12:4 and 12:12', ...]. A cure simulation applying exactly these deletions read passing on the current tool. No shared replacement sentence is prescribed: a simulation that appended one identical pilcrow sentence to 26 entries made the repeated-phrase check read 26-entry offending grams. If removing a mark mention leaves a seam with no wording, grammar or device ground, the author stops and reports to the editor instead of writing new seam evidence.

A standing order issued in round 1 for the whole book:

> a standing order

> A: an element of the evidence list matching ^\s*web: and matching (?i)WEB paragraph|paragraph start|the mark disclosure. B: a sentence (split on (?<=[.;])\s+) of the stated reason for the boundary, the strongest alternative considered or the formula notes matching (?i)pilcrow|WEB paragraph|paragraph start|chapter division and also matching (?i)corroborat|agree|join|inform|favour|support|lean|confirm.

> the evidence list, the stated reason for the boundary, the strongest alternative considered, the formula notes; all 70 entries of a working file

> Every A hit is deleted (a web: ref is never boundary evidence, rule 4). Every B hit is classified support-use (a WEB paragraph or chapter division informing, agreeing, joining, corroborating, leaning, favouring, supporting or confirming; or a pilcrow agreeing, joining, corroborating or confirming) or compliant (a disclaimer that the mark does not drive the seam, or an RP pilcrow that informs without deciding). Support-use clauses are deleted by the owning part. If a deletion leaves the evidence list below what the set of automated checks accepts, the author stops and reports.

> Baseline counted on pinned entries (a working file): A = 30 refs in 17 entries (drafting part 4 6 in 3 entries; drafting part 5 1 in 1 entry; drafting part 6 23 in 13 entries: 1 Corinthians 15:1–11..1 Corinthians 16:21–22). A W hit is disclosure only if a wording pattern matches the unit; it is location only if the unit is the observed signals item and VERB does not match the unit with the W matches removed; otherwise it is support use (presence and absence forms alike). A P hit is support use if a wording pattern matches, and informs compliant if only a wording pattern matches; otherwise it is not listed. In entries 1 Corinthians 12:1–3, 002, 003, 010, 011, 012, 013, 014 and 1 Corinthians 15:12–19, 003, 008, 011, 012 (round-1 a numbered review finding/a numbered review finding), every P mention other than the first the observed signals P item is count excess.

A true hit gets an author revision order naming the entry, field and span, recast to a location or disclosure wording; location and false positive hits are recorded with no change. It adds over a standing order: opens/closes/decides/drives/begins with a pilcrow subject, the units a wording pattern cannot see and a wording pattern does not cover (including a disclaimer in the same unit, e.g. 'opens the unit and decides nothing').

A standing order issued in round 4 for the whole book:

> a standing order

> For each locator matching (?<![\w:])ra working path(\d+)\.(\d+)(?!-) in a field, take the Greek run immediately after it (allowing one optional [TAG], then an opening parenthesis or double quote) or immediately before it where the locator is preceded by ' ('. The verse must be in pmarks rp sbl differences. Using a skeleton (NFD, casefold, final sigma to sigma, letters alpha to omega only), the quote hits if its token sequence is not contiguous in the SBL verse (kind wording absent from sbl) or is contiguous with a token flagged by an SBL sign (kind on sign: the whitespace token carrying ⸀ or ⸁, the tokens from ⸂ to ⸃ or from ⸄ to ⸅ inclusive, the token carrying ⸆, ⸇ or ⸈ and the next token). A hit is open unless the entry holds the exact token sbl:1 Corinthians.C.V for that verse anywhere; a range token holding the verse does not clear it. One hit per entry, verse and token sequence.

> the stated reason for the boundary, the strongest alternative considered, each element of the evidence list. the formula notes is excluded because it is not a warrant field.

> revision round 3 adds one the evidence list element per true hit in the entry's local form, giving the SBL reading, under each entry's named order. Widening over a numbered rule: a numbered rule requires the quoted words to fall on or across a sign; this also takes quotes whose words are not contiguous in the SBL verse though no sign falls on them, the rp sbl note class. False positives are classed by hand, only where the difference is orthography (elision, spelling) or the quote carries no seam or rival argument (a section of the reading plan rule 2), with the reason recorded per hit. Nothing is narrowed.

## 8. Licence and attribution

These are the book's own statements about its witnesses, quoted. None is a fresh reading of a witness's own distribution.

> Robinson-Pierpont Byzantine Textform (RP), the primary Greek text

> SBLGNT, the secondary Greek text (inline critical signs U+2E00-U+2E05 kept)

> World English Bible (WEB), the English text

> an owner directive: Byzantine primary, SBLGNT secondary; neither is preferred here

The licence of the primary Greek witness, as the section-mark layer records it:

> Unlicense (read from the source)

The licence and attribution of the secondary Greek witness, as the section-mark layer records them:

> CC BY 4.0; attribution: Copyright 2010 by the Society of Biblical Literature and Logos Bible Software

The attribution lines the formula inventory requires with any quotation:

> Greek witnesses: Robinson-Pierpont Byzantine Textform (Unlicense), primary; SBLGNT (CC BY 4.0), secondary; their attribution terms travel with any quotation

> World English Bible (WEB); its attribution terms travel with any quotation

The reading plan's own rule for carrying them:

> Attribution lines (read from the source, device inventory) travel with every quotation:

> - Greek witnesses: Robinson-Pierpont Byzantine Textform (Unlicense), primary; SBLGNT (CC BY 4.0), secondary; their attribution terms travel with any quotation.

> - World English Bible (WEB); its attribution terms travel with any quotation.

> 14. Carry both Greek attribution lines with any Greek quotation: Robinson-Pierpont Byzantine Textform (Unlicense); SBLGNT (CC BY 4.0).

A limit on the statements above. The book's own files state no licence term for the World English Bible: they name it and say that its attribution terms travel with any quotation, and they do not say what those terms are. The witness text files themselves state no terms: a search of the three of them for the words licence, public domain, Creative Commons or copyright found 0 occurrences. The terms should be confirmed against each witness's own distribution before this record is published. This record makes no claim on any witness beyond quotation for the purpose of showing what was argued from it.

## 10. The apparatus's own words

Every sentence in this record that is not a quotation was assembled from one of the following strings. A mark such as %d or %s stands where a count or a value from the reports was inserted.

- # The division of %s into reading units: a record of what was disputed
- This record is generated mechanically from the reports of the readers who examined a proposed division of %s into %d reading units. It is written for a reader of the book, not for a reader of the project that produced it: passages are named by chapter and verse.
- Every quotation set off with a rule at the left is a reader's own sentence, altered only by the mechanical substitutions listed in full near the end. Every other sentence is the generator's own frame, and every such sentence is printed in the last section so that the frame can be inspected apart from the evidence.
- This record makes no judgement of its own. It changes no unit, weighs no boundary and settles no dispute; it reports what the readers wrote. Its limits are stated in section 2 and are not small.

- ## 2. How the division was examined, and what that does and does not establish

- What this does not establish. Every reading at every stage was produced by one family of language model. Where two readings agree, that is one voice heard twice, not two independent witnesses, and agreement in this record must never be taken as confirmation. No report by a human scholar is among the listed files. Whether any reader consulted anything beyond the witnesses named in section 8 is not tested by this record.
- The readings were not all of one kind. Reading A examined %s of the %s groups of units, Reading B %s and Reading C %s.
- No reconciling reading exists for this book: where first readings differ, this record shows them side by side, then whatever the adjudicator ruled. Each review between revision rounds is one reader's report, not a pair.
- The first readings read the entries as they stood after the second revision round, not as they stand now. The entry's words quoted in section 3 are the final words; a reading's words may answer an earlier wording. The revision logs of section 4 show what moved.
- Of the %d files the project lists as landed, %d were opened and quoted, %d were opened and checked but not quoted, and %d were not opened (closing messages, named by the listed digest only). Section 7 lists every one. The project's records also name %d further outputs that have no durable file at any named place, and %d completion records that list no digest; those outputs could not be read by anyone and are not represented here beyond the list in section 7.
